94	      "cloudRunBackend": "backend/compute/cloud_run.py implements CloudRunCompute(ComputeBackend) (cloud_run.py:82-156). DISPATCH/SUBMIT: run_infer (cloud_run.py:103-132) builds worker argv [\"infer\",\"--video-uri\",...,\"--out-uri\",...,\"--done-uri\", f\"{out_root}/_dispatch/{token}.done\"] (token = injected self._token or fresh uuid4 per call, cloud_run.py:105-108), appends --segmenter and every --set k=v from spec.config_overrides PLUS _DEFAULT_CONTAINER_OVERRIDES = (\"deployment.compute_target=local_gpu\",\"indexing.detector_impl=native\") (cloud_run.py:41-44, 109-112) so the dispatched container runs INLINE and never re-dispatches, then calls self._client.run_job(job_name, argv, region, project) (cloud_run.py:114-115). POLL/AWAIT: marker = poll_until(lambda: self._read_marker(done_uri), self._policy, label=\"cloud-run-infer\") (cloud_run.py:119-120) using execution_policy.poll_until (execution_policy.py:125-151; default poll_every_n_sec=10s, max_wait_sec=1800s, raises PolicyTimeout on exhaustion). COMPLETION DETECTION IS A BUCKET-POLL of a storage marker, NOT the Cloud Run API status: _read_marker (cloud_run.py:135-140) parse_uri(done_uri) then get_storage(ref.backend).exists(ref), returning None (keep polling) until the worker writes the marker; once present it reads the JSON (_read_json, cloud_run.py:142-155, downloads to a temp local dst first since gcs/s3 require an explicit dst). Then it extracts video_id + returncode (a MISSING returncode defaults to FAILURE 1, never 0; an empty/unreadable marker is treated as failure, cloud_run.py:121-128), composes outputs_uri = f\"{out_root}/outputs/{video_id}/\", reads report.json, returns ComputeResult (cloud_run.py:129-132). The marker is written by the worker via _write_done_marker (backend/worker/__main__.py:140-153, {video_id, returncode, segment_count, status} pushed with storage.put), which CloudRunCompute consumes — so the completion signal is storage-backed (D16), reusing the existing park/resume primitives.",
95	      "wiring": "The compute seam is wired into the STANDALONE CLI worker ONLY, NOT into /api/process. backend/worker/__main__.py::cmd_infer (worker/__main__.py:156-168) calls compute = get_compute_backend(config) and, if non-None, returns _dispatch_remote(compute, args) (worker/__main__.py:116-137), which builds a ComputeJobSpec from argv and calls compute.run_infer(spec), mapping PolicyTimeout -> rc 4 (worker/__main__.py:124-137); if None it falls through to the unchanged inline PULL->hash->validate->segment path (worker/__main__.py:170+). This worker is `python -m backend.worker infer ...` — the same binary the Cloud Run container runs. CRITICAL GAP: the HTTP request/job flow does NOT use this seam. POST /api/process (backend/main.py:268-308) only fast-fails + creates a 'queued' jobs-table row and calls _get_executor().submit(_drain_due_jobs) (main.py:304). _drain_due_jobs (backend/api/job_worker.py:132-153) -> _run_claimed_job (job_worker.py:72-98) -> backend.main._execute_processing (main.py:148+) runs the pipeline IN-PROCESS on the ThreadPoolExecutor (job_worker.py:30-38, max_workers=1). _execute_processing NEVER calls get_compute_backend / compute.run_infer — it always hashes/validates/segments locally in the API process. So today every /api/process job runs in-process; the Cloud Run dispatch path is reachable only by invoking the backend.worker CLI directly with a cloud_run profile. No dispatcher bridges the API job worker to the ComputeBackend yet.",
96	      "flagsDefault": "Selection is config.deployment.compute_target (Literal[\"\", \"local_gpu\", \"cloud_run\", \"cpu_mock\"], DEFAULT \"\" — backend/config/models.py:351). get_compute_backend returns non-None ONLY for compute_target == \"cloud_run\" (__init__.py:47-49); \"\", \"local_gpu\", \"cpu_mock\" all return None => in-process/inline. compute_target is normally set via deployment.profile (Literal[\"\", \"truly-local\", \"cloud-serving\", \"cpu-mock\"], DEFAULT \"\" — models.py:348), whose presets (backend/config/presets.py:43-63; COMPUTE_TARGET_FOR_PROFILE presets.py:32-36) map cloud-serving->cloud_run, truly-local->local_gpu, cpu-mock->cpu_mock; an explicitly-blanked compute_target under an active profile is back-filled to the profile's canonical target by the loader (backend/config/loader.py:167-181). CONFIRMED DEFAULT-OFF: with no profile selected both profile and compute_target are \"\" (models.py:348,351; presets.py:11-16), so get_compute_backend returns None and the worker runs inline — Cloud Run is default-OFF and in-process is the default. Cloud Run coordinates come from ENV not config: CLOUD_RUN_JOB (default \"rally-worker\"), CLOUD_RUN_REGION (default \"asia-south1\"), GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT (__init__.py:54-57). Related M8 BillingConfig.enabled is also default-OFF (models.py:367).",
97	      "mockedVsReal": "REAL: CloudRunCompute orchestration (argv build, container-override injection, bucket-poll loop, marker parsing with fail-safe returncode, report fetch) is real and unit-testable; storage layer (parse_uri/get_storage/fetch/put/exists) is real; the worker's _write_done_marker (worker/__main__.py:140-153) is real; config/profile/loader plumbing is real. The real client GoogleCloudRunJobClient.run_job (cloud_run.py:60-79) DOES contain real Cloud Run API calls when reached — it lazy-imports google.cloud.run_v2, builds JobsClient().job_path(project,region,job_name), wraps argv in RunJobRequest.Overrides.ContainerOverride(args=argv), and calls client.run_job(RunJobRequest(...)) — BUT it is marked '# pragma: no cover - exercised only on the real M7 run' and google-cloud-run is intentionally NOT a CI/test dependency (cloud_run.py:60-72), so this path has never actually run; owner-verification is deferred to M7 (__init__.py:45, cloud_run.py:18-19). MOCKED IN PRACTICE: the trigger is an injected abstraction CloudRunJobClient (ABC, cloud_run.py:47-57); tests pass a FAKE client (no GCP) and that is the ONLY exercised path. A real cloud dispatch still needs: (1) install google-cloud-run + valid GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT/region creds on the control plane; (2) a deployed Cloud Run GPU Job named per CLOUD_RUN_JOB whose container runs `python -m backend.worker infer`; (3) a real gcs storage backend reachable by both dispatcher and container for the gs:// video/out/done URIs; (4) owner-verification that ContainerOverride(args=argv) truly overrides job args and the container writes the done-marker; (5) crucially, an API-side dispatcher to route /api/process jobs through the ComputeBackend (bypassed today); (6) M8 usage-emission hooks (cost stays 0 without them, job_worker.py:101-129).",

Cloud Run dispatch is reachable only by invoking `python -m backend.worker infer` directly with a cloud_run config; no dispatcher bridges the async job worker to the ComputeBackend.",

CI only runs the injected fake client.",

102	        "COMPLETION SIGNAL IS A STORAGE MARKER, NOT CLOUD RUN STATUS (by design, D16): a container that dies BEFORE writing the marker is detected only by poll_until exhausting max_wait_sec=1800s -> PolicyTimeout -> rc 4 (cloud_run.py:119-120, worker/__main__.py:124-134); the Cloud Run execution's own failure status is never consulted, so a fast-failing job still costs the full ~30min poll budget before being reported failed.",

107	    "deploy": {
108	      "dockerfile": "deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile (C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer/deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile, 62 lines) builds a single image for the rally GPU **Job** worker (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M6/M7, ADR-012/D16).\n\nBASE IMAGE: `nvidia/cuda:12.4.1-runtime-ubuntu22.04` (CUDA 12.4 runtime, Ubuntu 22.04) — a GPU/CUDA base, not a slim/CPU base.\n\nOS DEPS: apt-installs `ffmpeg` (decode/stitch), `fonts-dejavu-core` (watermark filter), `git`/`curl`/`ca-certificates`/`build-essential`, and the distro `python3`/`python3-pip`/`python3-venv`.\n\nTWO DELIBERATELY-SEPARATE PYTHON STACKS (same split as the native GPU box deploy/digitalocean/gpu_setup.sh, to avoid a framework/torch conflict):\n  1) MAIN APP ENV — system python3; `pip install .` of this repo from pyproject.toml (COPY pyproject.toml, README.md, backend/, training/). Drives the pipeline + ffmpeg stitching. Notably the main env installs NO torch (detection is delegated out to the WASB subprocess).\n  2) NATIVE WASB CONDA ENV — installs Miniconda to /opt/conda, accepts conda TOS for main+r channels, creates conda env `wasb` with python=3.8, clones https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT.git into /opt/models/WASB-SBDT, and pip-installs torch==1.11.0+cu113 / torchvision==0.12.0+cu113 (CUDA 11.3 wheels, via the pytorch cu113 extra-index) plus hydra-core, omegaconf, pandas, opencv-python-headless, pillow, scipy, tqdm, pyyaml, matplotlib, numpy<1.24, and the WASB repo's own requirements.txt if present. The native runner shells out to $WASB_PYTHON as a subprocess, so the app's torch never co-installs with the detector's torch-1.11 stack.\n\nWASB WEIGHTS: NOT baked in (WASB-C3 — pretrained badminton weights are academic-data-trained, provenance not cleared for commercial sharing). The image only sets $WASB_WEIGHTS to a default path (/opt/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar); weights are expected to be staged at runtime (bucket fetch or mount) and pointed at via $WASB_WEIGHTS.\n\nENV WIRING: WASB_PYTHON=/opt/conda/envs/wasb/bin/python, WASB_REPO_DIR=/opt/models/WASB-SBDT, WASB_WEIGHTS=<default path>, RALLY_DETECTOR_IMPL=native (so the container uses the Linux-native WASB runner, not the WSL/cuda path). The native runner finds the env+repo purely by these env vars (no `conda activate`).\n\nENTRYPOINT: `[\"python3\", \"-m\", \"backend.worker\"]` — i.e. the WORKER, not the FastAPI server. The dispatcher supplies per-execution args at run time: `infer --video-uri gs://… --out-uri gs://… --done-uri gs://… --segmenter wasb_hybrid --set …`. backend/worker/__main__.py cmd_infer runs detect+window+stitch INLINE (co-located so CPU/wall/egress meter into one job, D4), then writes the report and (when --done-uri is passed) a tiny JSON completion marker {video_id, returncode, ...} to the bucket.",
109	      "buildable": "SCAFFOLD / owner-built-once, NOT built by CI and not part of the automated test loop. Findings: (1) NO CI builds it — the only GitHub Actions workflow is .github/workflows/ci.yml, which runs the mocked offline test suite on a CPU runner (it even installs CPU-only torch and explicitly notes torch is not a pyproject dep); a repo-wide grep for `gcloud builds submit`/`gcloud run`/`buildx`/`docker build` finds zero hits in CI. The Dockerfile header states plainly: \"CI does NOT build this (a CUDA image is too heavy)\". (2) NO build/push/deploy SCRIPT exists — there is no shell script that builds or pushes this image. The deploy/gcp/*.sh scripts (provision.sh, create_gpu_vm.sh, ops-agent-startup.sh, teardown.sh) only provision a GPU VM + GCS bucket + IAM + budget; none reference Cloud Run, the Dockerfile, or `gcloud run`/`gcloud builds`. The only build/push/deploy instructions are COPY-PASTE commands documented in deploy/cloudrun/README.md (gcloud builds submit; gcloud run jobs create), to be run by the owner. (3) The control-plane dispatch shim IS real + landed + mock-tested (backend/compute/cloud_run.py CloudRunCompute, tests/test_compute_backend.py with a fake CloudRunJobClient — the real GoogleCloudRunJobClient lazy-imports google-cloud-run, which is intentionally NOT a test dependency, so that path is never exercised in CI). Per the README/Dockerfile, the real build+push+run on an L4 is the **M7 owner step** within a $100/₹10k budget; status in deploy/cloudrun/README.md is \"dispatch shim + image are landed + mock-tested (M6); the real build + push + run on an L4 is the M7 owner step.\" So: the image is intended to be buildable and the worker/dispatch logic is exercised by tests, but the image itself is verified only by a manual owner run, not by any automated build/push/deploy/CI.",
110	      "gpuStory": "The image is GPU-FIRST and Cloud-Run-Job-only. Base is the CUDA 12.4 runtime and the WASB conda env pins torch 1.11.0+cu113 / torchvision 0.12.0+cu113 (CUDA wheels), so the detector subprocess expects an NVIDIA GPU. The Cloud Run resource it targets is a GPU **Job** (not a Service): deploy/cloudrun/README.md §2 creates it with `gcloud run jobs create rally-worker --gpu 1 --gpu-type nvidia-l4 --cpu 4 --memory 16Gi --max-retries 0 --task-timeout 3600`, scale-to-zero (D7, no warm pool); the control plane bucket-polls a done-marker rather than the Cloud Run API. CPU-vs-GPU at the IMAGE/deploy level is NOT a configurable axis here — there is no CPU variant of this image, no `--gpu`-optional path, and ffmpeg is NVDEC-decode + libx264-encode (the image keeps ffmpeg's default libx264 for parity, D16). A CPU FALLBACK does exist but only at the CODE level, not in the container build: the current branch's M4 work (feat/serving-m4-device-context, commit 6932d4a) added a DeviceContext (backend/pipeline/detectors/device.py) and native WASB CPU-fallback where device=\"auto\" can degrade to CPU while device=\"cuda\" stays strict. That fallback is a property of the native runner / config, not something this Dockerfile's cu113 torch build or the L4 Job spec is set up to exploit — the cu113 wheels would still run on CPU only via torch's CPU code paths if no GPU were present, but the documented serving path always requests an L4. In short: native WASB packaging into a CUDA container is feasible and is exactly what this Dockerfile does (conda py3.8 + torch-1.11+cu113 mirroring the verified DO/native GPU box); the open caveat is weights provenance (WASB-C3), not GPU packaging.",
111	      "deployScripts": "NONE beyond the Dockerfile + manual README commands. deploy/cloudrun/ contains only Dockerfile + README.md — no build.sh/deploy.sh/cloudbuild.yaml. The actual Cloud Run lifecycle lives as documented copy-paste commands in deploy/cloudrun/README.md: §1 `gcloud artifacts repositories create … && gcloud builds submit --tag …:latest -f deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile .`; §2 `gcloud run jobs create rally-worker --gpu 1 --gpu-type nvidia-l4 --cpu 4 --memory 16Gi --max-retries 0 --task-timeout 3600 --set-env-vars WASB_WEIGHTS=…`; §3 wiring the dispatcher via env (DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE=cloud-serving → compute_target=cloud_run, CLOUD_RUN_JOB / CLOUD_RUN_REGION / GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT). The deploy/gcp/ scripts are unrelated to Cloud Run — provision.sh (GCS bucket + rally-worker SA + Storage Object Admin + Ops-Agent writer roles + $100 budget + GPU-quota check), create_gpu_vm.sh (spins up a g2-standard-4 L4 / n1+T4 VM with the Ops Agent — a plain GPU VM, NOT Cloud Run), ops-agent-startup.sh, teardown.sh (defaults to delete). There is no `gcloud run deploy` or `gcloud run jobs` anywhere in deploy/gcp/ (grep-confirmed). M7 acceptance is a manual owner runlog (DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_<date>.md under docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/), not an automated deploy.",

117	        "Known correctness gap documented in the README: a FAILED Job writes no done-marker, so the dispatcher's bounded bucket-poll just times out to a generic rc 4 and cannot distinguish a real failure from a slow cold-start; a failure-marker carrying the real rc is a noted follow-up.",

It realizes Cloud Run + GPU (L4) scale-to-zero JOBS for pay-per-video serving via one pure DETECT/WINDOW/STITCH core selected by a ComputeBackend (where) + StorageBackend (what-bytes) at startup config. The code seam lives in backend/compute/ (the CloudRunCompute dispatch shim) and the container in deploy/cloudrun/. And yes — it is very actively being worked: this is the current concurrent serving session's primary focus, which shipped ~13 default-OFF PRs (M0→M8, #278–#288) in roughly 7 hours on 2026-06-21 and has PR #290 (M9 edge-auth) open and non-draft right now. The Cloud Run scan is back with a definitive answer. The broader all-docs next-steps scan is still finishing — here's the serving-track piece now, then I'll fold it into the full roadmap.

## Where the Cloud-Run work lives — and yes, it's actively being built

**Source of truth:** [`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/README.md`](docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/README.md) §7 tracker + §11 changelog, ratified by **ADR-011/012/013/014** ([DECISIONS.md](docs/archives/decisions/DECISIONS.md)).

| Layer | Where | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Design** | ADR-011/012/014 + COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/README | ✅ ratified (Cloud Run **Jobs** + L4 GPU, scale-to-zero, per-video billing) |
| **Code seam** | `backend/compute/{base.py, __init__.py, cloud_run.py}` | `CloudRunCompute` dispatch shim — **landed default-OFF** (M6 #287); dispatches a Job + bucket-polls a `.done` marker |
| **Container** | `deploy/cloudrun/{Dockerfile, README.md}` | image exists (CUDA 12.4 + ffmpeg + a conda `wasb` torch-1.11 subprocess) — **not CI-built**; real build = M7 |
| **Config** | `backend/config/{models,presets,loader}.py` | `deployment.profile` / `compute_target` — default `""` = **in-process (OFF)** |

**Actively worked? Decisively yes — it's the concurrent session's head-of-line work.** ~13 isolated default-OFF PRs took it from **M0 → M8 in ~7 hours today** (#278→#288, +my #289 fix), and **M9 edge-auth is OPEN right now as [#290](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/290)**.

**Milestone status:** M1–M4 ✅ (config/input/output/device foundation, merged) · M5/M6/M8 ◐ (code landed default-OFF, gated on the real GPU run/spend) · **M7** (real L4 e2e) + M9–M12 ☐ owner-gated.

**The one big functional gap** (and the most interesting un-owned piece): the **HTTP path isn't wired to the seam yet**. `POST /api/process → _drain_due_jobs → _execute_processing` always runs **in-process**; `get_compute_backend()` is consulted **only** by the standalone `python -m backend.worker infer` CLI. So Cloud Run dispatch works from the worker CLI but not from the API job worker — bridging that (an API→`ComputeBackend` dispatcher) is net-new and currently un-owned.

**⚠ Don't duplicate / coordinate:** M0–M8 are done by the active session and M9 is in open PR #290 — re-doing any of it (or hand-rolling a second Cloud Run client / container) would collide on this shared checkout. The serving track is owner-driven, one default-OFF PR per milestone off `origin/master`.

The full cross-track next-steps roadmap (appliance/CUJ, localization, rally-quality, owned-model) is landing from the second scan — I'll consolidate everything into one prioritized list when it completes.

# Localization Research: Adding 7 Languages (Thai, Indonesian, Danish, Spanish, Chinese, Japanese, Korean)

**Scope:** Extends the existing `en/kn/hi` i18n foundation (react-i18next + i18next-icu, ICU MessageFormat, self-hosted OFL Noto fonts, CI key-parity, native-speaker review) per `docs/I18N_PLAN.md`. All facts below are sourced to authoritative references (CLDR v48 chart, SIL OFL, @fontsource, W3C/BCP-47, notofonts).

## Key headline findings

- **All 7 are LTR.** No RTL layout work is needed (relevant because your current `hi`/`kn` are also LTR — no new bidi handling).
- **Spanish now has 3 cardinal plural forms** in current CLDR: `{one, many, other}`. The `many` form is **not** a colloquial plural — it triggers only on large/compact numbers (millions, scientific notation). This is the single most important authoring gotcha here.
- **CJK + Thai need dedicated OFL Noto fonts; the 3 Latin-script languages (es, da, id) need no new font** — they're covered by your existing Latin UI font.
- **CJK fonts are huge** (~7–16 MB per weight unsubsetted). This is the dominant cost driver and the reason CJK should be phased last with lazy-loading.

## Per-language table

| Lang | BCP-47 code | CLDR **cardinal** plural categories | ICU authoring | Dir | Script | Dedicated font? | OFL? | @fontsource pkg | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Thai** | `th` | **`other`** (single-form) | one form only | LTR | Thai | **Noto Sans Thai** | ✅ OFL-1.1 | `@fontsource-variable/noto-sans-thai` | Small font (~Latin-ish, single script block). No-space word-wrap gotcha (below). |
| **Indonesian** | `id` | **`other`** (single-form) | one form only | LTR | Latin | **No** (Latin) | n/a | (uses existing Latin font) | Pure catalog add. |
| **Danish** | `da` | **`one, other`** | 2 forms | LTR | Latin | **No** (Latin) | n/a | (uses existing Latin font) | Same shape as English; `one` rule includes decimals (`n=1 or t!=0 and i=0,1`). |
| **Spanish** | `es` | **`one, many, other`** ⚠ | **3 forms** | LTR | Latin | **No** (Latin) | n/a | (uses existing Latin font) | `many` = millions/compact only. ~15–30% string expansion vs English. |
| **Chinese** | **`zh-Hans`** (decide vs `zh-Hant`) | **`other`** (single-form) | one form only | LTR | Han (Simplified) | **Noto Sans SC** | ✅ OFL-1.1 | `@fontsource-variable/noto-sans-sc` | **Script-variant decision required** (below). Large font. |
| **Japanese** | `ja` | **`other`** (single-form) | one form only | LTR | Han+Kana | **Noto Sans JP** | ✅ OFL-1.1 | `@fontsource-variable/noto-sans-jp` | No region subtag needed. Large font. |
| **Korean** | `ko` | **`other`** (single-form) | one form only | LTR | Hangul | **Noto Sans KR** | ✅ OFL-1.1 | `@fontsource-variable/noto-sans-kr` | No region subtag needed. Large font (Hangul syllable blocks). |

### 1. BCP-47 codes + script-variant decisions
- **`th`, `id`, `da`, `es`, `ja`, `ko`** — plain language subtag, **no script or region subtag needed**. (Japanese and Korean each map to one script; no decision to make.)
- **Chinese — the one real decision.** Per W3C BCP-47 guidance, when the distinction is *script* (Simplified vs Traditional), use the **script subtag**, not a region code: prefer **`zh-Hans`** (Simplified) over `zh-CN`, and **`zh-Hant`** (Traditional) over `zh-TW`. This is the documented, increasingly-standard choice and is region-independent.
  - **Recommendation:** ship **`zh-Hans`** first (Simplified — mainland + Singapore, the larger audience and your most likely market). Add **`zh-Hant`** later only if Taiwan/Hong Kong demand appears — it's a *separate catalog + a separate font (Noto Sans TC)*, not a free variant, because the character sets and many word choices differ. Do **not** label your catalog bare `zh`; key it `zh-Hans` so a future `zh-Hant` slots in cleanly under your existing supported-locales list.

### 2. CLDR cardinal plural categories (CLDR v48 chart — authoritative)
- **Single-form (`other` only):** Thai, Indonesian, Chinese, Japanese, Korean. ICU authoring is trivial — a single `other {…}` arm; these languages do not grammatically inflect for number.
- **Two-form (`one, other`):** Danish — same authoring shape as English. (CLDR `one` rule: `n = 1 or t != 0 and i = 0,1`.)
- **Three-form (`one, many, other`):** **Spanish** ⚠. The `many` arm exists in current CLDR (rule: triggers for integer millions / `e` (compact-exponent) cases — `i % 1000000 = 0 and v = 0`, or compact exponent `e ∈ 1..5`). Practically: `1 → one`, `2 → other`, `1000000 → many`. **Every Spanish ICU plural message must include a `many {…}` arm**, even if it usually mirrors `other`, or i18next-icu/intl-messageformat will fall back. This is recent enough that older translation tooling and many hand-written catalogs omit it — flag it explicitly to your native-speaker reviewer and add it to the key-parity/lint check for `es`.

### 3. Text direction
**All 7 are LTR.** Confirmed — no RTL/bidi layout work, no `dir="rtl"` mirroring, no logical-property CSS audit needed for this batch. (If Arabic/Hebrew/Urdu ever come, that's a separate, much larger track.)

### 4. Fonts
**No new font (covered by your existing Latin UI font):** Spanish, Danish, Indonesian — these are pure catalog adds (Latin script, incl. Spanish `ñ á í ó ú ü ¿ ¡` and Danish `æ ø å`, all in any standard Latin font's Latin-1/Latin Extended coverage).

**Dedicated Noto fonts (all OFL-1.1 → commercially bundleable under your §4.2(a) OFL-for-media exception; all on `@fontsource`/`@fontsource-variable`):**

| Font | Script | OFL | Bundling | Size concern |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Noto Sans Thai** | Thai | ✅ OFL-1.1 | bundle freely | **Small** — single Thai block (~few hundred glyphs); comparable to a Latin webfont. No subsetting headache. |
| **Noto Sans SC** | Simplified Chinese | ✅ OFL-1.1 | bundle freely | **Very large** — full Noto Sans CJK SC is ~**16 MB per weight** unsubsetted (OTF), near the 65,535-glyph OpenType ceiling. |
| **Noto Sans JP** | Japanese | ✅ OFL-1.1 | bundle freely | **Very large** — same CJK family, comparable order of magnitude (~7–16 MB/weight depending on format). |
| **Noto Sans KR** | Korean | ✅ OFL-1.1 | bundle freely | **Very large** — ~11,000+ Hangul syllable blocks plus Han; multi-MB/weight. |

**Licensing:** All Noto fonts (incl. the CJK family) are **SIL Open Font License 1.1**. Google/SIL explicitly invite bundling into apps and devices; the only OFL restriction is you can't sell the fonts *standalone*. This fits cleanly under the existing `docs/I18N_PLAN.md` §4.2(a) "OFL-for-bundled-media" exception you already carved out for Noto Sans Kannada/Devanagari — no new licensing decision required, just extend the same exception to these 4.

**The CJK subsetting concern (critical for an offline / air-gapped self-hosted bundle):**
- `@fontsource` handles CJK by **splitting each font into many small `unicode-range`-keyed `woff2` chunks** (Latin fonts split into ~a handful; **CJK fonts split into ~100+ chunks** spanning the Han/Kana/Hangul ranges). The browser fetches only the chunks whose code points actually appear on screen — so a typical UI loads a small fraction, not the whole 16 MB.
- **But for an offline/air-gapped bundle this lazy-by-unicode-range model breaks down:** you must ship **the entire chunk set** so any string renders without a network fetch. Plan for **~5–16 MB per CJK language per weight on disk** in the bundle. With 3 CJK languages (SC, JP, KR) × even 1–2 weights, that's **tens of MB** added to an otherwise small bundle — this is the dominant size cost of this whole project and the reason CJK is phased last.
- **Mitigations:** (a) use the **variable** `@fontsource-variable` packages (one file spans all weights, avoids N× per-weight duplication); (b) consider **building a custom subset** restricted to the glyphs your reviewed `zh-Hans/ja/ko` catalogs + dynamic data (rally counts, dates) actually use — for a small fixed UI this can cut a CJK font from MBs to tens of KB, at the cost of a build step that must re-run whenever catalog text changes; (c) **lazy-load CJK font CSS per active locale** so non-CJK users never download it (works for online; for true air-gapped you still ship everything but only load on demand).

### 5. Rendering / layout gotchas
- **Thai — no spaces between words.** Thai uses spaces as *phrase* delimiters, not word delimiters, so naive wrapping breaks mid-word. Browsers do dictionary-based line breaking but it's imperfect. Mitigations: `word-break: normal` + `overflow-wrap: break-word` as a safety net, optionally `line-break: loose/auto`, and avoid forcing breaks. Thai also has stacking combining marks (vowels/tones above/below) — ensure line-height has headroom so diacritics aren't clipped.
- **CJK line-breaking** can break between (almost) any two characters, which is mostly fine, but watch **kinsoku** (forbidden line-start/end punctuation) — `line-break: strict` improves this. CJK glyphs are taller/denser; give generous `line-height`. CJK generally has **no italic** and reacts poorly to faux-bold — rely on the variable-weight axis, not `font-style: italic`.
- **Spanish string expansion** — expect **~15–30% longer** than English (and occasional longer single words). Buttons/labels/toasts must not be fixed-width or truncate; test the longest Spanish strings. (Your current `Rally Segment(s) Selected → t("compile.selected",{count})` plural key is exactly the kind of element to stress-test.) Danish is similar but milder; Indonesian is roughly English-length.
- **CJK/Thai are more *compact* horizontally** than Latin, so the risk is the opposite (lots of whitespace), not overflow — but ensure your layout doesn't assume a minimum string length either.

## Recommended phasing

Order by *least new code/assets first*, then *one script-block font*, then *the heavy CJK + script-variant decision last*:

- **Phase A — Latin trio (catalog-only, zero new code/assets): `es`, `da`, `id`.**
  No font, no lazy-loading, no bundle-size impact. The *only* engineering touch is making your ICU plural authoring + key-parity/lint check handle **Spanish's 3-form `{one, many, other}`** (Danish/Indonesian are 2-form/1-form and trivially fit existing tooling). Best first slice: highest ROI, proves the catalog pipeline scales beyond Indic without code changes. Spanish is the only one needing reviewer attention (the `many` arm).
- **Phase B — Thai (`th`): one new small OFL font.**
  Add **Noto Sans Thai** (`@fontsource-variable/noto-sans-thai`) — small, single-script, no subsetting drama; reuses the exact font-bundling pattern you already established for Noto Kannada/Devanagari. Single-form plural (`other`). Add the Thai no-space word-wrap CSS safety net. Self-contained, low-risk.
- **Phase C — CJK (`zh-Hans`, `ja`, `ko`): the heavy slice — needs new code/assets + a product decision.**
  Requires: (1) the **`zh-Hans` vs `zh-Hant` decision** (recommend ship `zh-Hans` only first); (2) **3 large OFL Noto fonts** (SC/JP/KR); (3) **per-locale lazy-loading of font CSS** so non-CJK users aren't penalized; (4) for the **offline/air-gapped bundle, a CJK subsetting build step** (or accept tens of MB). All single-form plural (`other`), so catalog authoring is the easy part — the work is fonts + loading, not strings. Do this last and as its own PR(s); consider one PR per CJK language given the font weight.

## Catalog-only vs new-code/asset summary

| Add | Catalog-only (code-free) | Needs new assets/code |
|---|---|---|
| `es` | catalog ✅ | minor: ICU `many` arm + lint/parity update for 3-form |
| `da` | catalog ✅ | none |
| `id` | catalog ✅ | none |
| `th` | catalog | **+1 OFL font** (Noto Sans Thai); Thai word-wrap CSS |
| `zh-Hans` | catalog | **+1 large OFL font** (Noto Sans SC) + lazy-load + script-variant decision + offline subsetting |
| `ja` | catalog | **+1 large OFL font** (Noto Sans JP) + lazy-load + offline subsetting |
| `ko` | catalog | **+1 large OFL font** (Noto Sans KR) + lazy-load + offline subsetting |

Your existing **extensibility checklist** (`add locales/<code>/common.json → i18n_draft → human review → add to supported-locales → confirm font coverage → CI key-parity green`) holds for all 7 — the only checklist step that does real work is **"confirm font coverage"** for `th`/`zh-Hans`/`ja`/`ko`, plus the **Spanish 3-form plural** wrinkle on the authoring side.

## Sources

- Unicode CLDR v48 — Language Plural Rules chart (cardinal categories per language): https://www.unicode.org/cldr/charts/48/supplemental/language_plural_rules.html
- Unicode CLDR — Plural Rules spec: https://cldr.unicode.org/index/cldr-spec/plural-rules
- W3C i18n — BCP-47 / Chinese script subtags (`zh-Hans`/`zh-Hant` over `zh-CN`/`zh-TW`): https://www.w3.org/International/articles/bcp47/ and https://www.w3.org/International/articles/language-tags/
- IETF language tag (BCP-47) overview: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IETF_language_tag
- SIL Open Font License (OFL) — fonts list & terms: https://openfontlicense.org/ofl-fonts/
- Noto CJK license (SIL OFL 1.1) — Google Open Source Blog + repo LICENSE: https://opensource.googleblog.com/2017/04/noto-serif-cjk-is-here.html and https://github.com/notofonts/noto-cjk/blob/main/Sans/LICENSE
- Noto CJK repo (SC/TC/HK/JP/KR variants, sizes): https://github.com/notofonts/noto-cjk and https://github.com/notofonts/noto-cjk/blob/main/Sans/README.md
- @fontsource variable packages (self-host, OFL): noto-sans-thai, noto-sans-sc, noto-sans-jp, noto-sans-kr — e.g. https://www.npmjs.com/package/@fontsource-variable/noto-sans-thai , https://fontsource.org/fonts/noto-sans-jp/install , https://fontsource.org/fonts/noto-sans-kr/install
- W3C — Thai line-breaking / no-space word wrap: https://w3c.github.io/i18n-drafts/articles/typography/linebreak.en and https://www.w3.org/TR/thai-gap/
- react-i18next + i18next-icu (ICU MessageFormat usage): https://react.i18next.com/misc/using-with-icu-format and https://github.com/i18next/i18next-icu

**Verification notes (honest scope):** CLDR categories, BCP-47 codes, OFL status, LTR, and @fontsource package availability are confirmed from the cited authoritative sources. **Exact self-hosted byte sizes** (the CJK ~7–16 MB/weight figures) are order-of-magnitude from notofonts/opensuse data — confirm the precise per-package `woff2` chunk totals by running `npm view @fontsource-variable/noto-sans-sc` and inspecting `node_modules` before committing bundle-budget numbers to the project doc. **Font rendering** (Thai diacritic clipping, CJK kinsoku) needs an eyeball on the owner's machine, same caveat as the existing Kannada/Devanagari verification step in §6.

A note for the project doc: this batch fits your existing foundation with **zero changes to the negotiation contract or catalog format** — the only foundation-level additions are (a) extending the OFL-for-media font exception to 4 more Noto fonts, (b) handling Spanish's 3-form plural in the ICU authoring + CI parity/lint check, and (c) a CJK lazy-load + offline-subset strategy. Everything else is "add a reviewed catalog," exactly as designed.

### 457. assistant (2026-06-21T14:15:04.206Z)

# Multilingual Expansion — 7 new locales (th · id · da · es · zh-Hans · ja · ko)

> **Status:** `DRAFT` — **owner-gated (approval needed to START this line)** · **Owner:** Avi Dullu · **Created:** 2026-06-21 · **Last updated:** 2026-06-21
> **Lifecycle:** `DRAFT → IN PROGRESS → DONE → archived` (move to `docs/archives/` when DONE)
> **Tracking anchors:** §7 progress tracker is the source of truth; index in `docs/README.md`; pointer in memory `current-state`; thread in `NEXT_STEPS.md` once work starts.
> **Relation to existing docs:** **PEER-OF / extends** [`FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md`](FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md) (the `en`/`kn`/`hi` foundation) and the engine design `I18N_PLAN.md`. **Reuses that foundation verbatim** (react-i18next + i18next-icu + browser detector + key-parity/no-bare-strings CI + self-hosted OFL Noto via `@fontsource` + Gemini-draft→native-review). This is a **parallel** line: it adds locales, it does **not** re-architect i18n.
> **Honesty note:** each claim tagged `[verified]` (checked vs the working tree this session), `[researched]` (sourced, needs sign-off), or `[design]` (proposed). Not legal advice — the OFL-font carve-out is the owner's standing call (D7 in FRIENDLY).

---

## 0. TL;DR
Add **Thai, Indonesian, Danish, Spanish, Chinese (Simplified), Japanese, Korean** to the Studio SPA with the **same rigor as en/kn/hi**. The good news `[verified]`: the i18n foundation already scales — **zero changes to the negotiation contract or the catalog format**; adding a locale is "drop a reviewed `common.json` + (if non-Latin) bundle one OFL Noto font + keep CI key-parity green." The work splits cleanly into **Phase A** (es/da/id — *catalog-only, no fonts*), **Phase B** (Thai — *one small OFL font*), and **Phase C** (zh-Hans/ja/ko — *large OFL CJK fonts + lazy-load + an offline-bundle subsetting decision + the zh script-variant call*). **The single most important caveats:** (1) **native-speaker review per language** is the real cost and the public-launch gate (Gemini drafts are *inference-only*, never the source of truth — the standing guardrail); (2) **CJK fonts add tens of MB** to the otherwise-tiny air-gapped bundle; (3) **Spanish now has a 3-form plural** (`{one, many, other}`) — the one authoring wrinkle.

## 1. Problem & goal
**Why now.** en/kn/hi shipped and proved the foundation is locale-pluggable (a Kannada-default browser auto-renders Kannada; the switcher walks kn→hi→en). The owner wants to broaden reach beyond the India beachhead — **SE Asia (th, id), Europe (da, es), and East Asia (zh, ja, ko)**.
**Concrete outcome ("good"):** a user whose browser is in any of the 7 languages lands on the Studio (and, once `site/` is localized via FRIENDLY B3, the marketing pages too), and — without help — understands the product, runs the tool, and gets a reel, **entirely in their language**, with the fonts rendering crisply offline.
**Read to get current:** this doc §3–§4; [`FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md`](FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md) (the foundation this rides on, esp. §4 design + §6.5 negotiation tests); `web/src/i18n/index.ts` (the detector/ICU/fallback config) + `web/src/main.tsx` (the `@fontsource` font-load pattern) + `web/src/i18n/hygiene.test.ts` (key-parity + no-bare-strings + offline guards).

## 2. Decisions locked (inherited from FRIENDLY unless noted)
| # | Decision | Source / date | Implication |
|---|----------|---------------|-------------|
| ED1 | **Same framework, no re-architecture** — react-i18next + i18next-icu + `i18next-browser-languagedetector`; one `en` source catalog + per-locale siblings; **always fall back to `en`** | FRIENDLY D-* / `i18n/index.ts` `[verified]` | Each new locale = add to `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` + a `common.json`; negotiation/format unchanged |
| ED2 | **Self-host OFL Noto via `@fontsource`** (the D7 OFL-for-media carve-out) — **extend it to the 4 new Noto fonts** (Thai, SC, JP, KR) | FRIENDLY D7 (owner, 2026-06-21) | No new licensing decision; same `main.tsx` import + `unicode-range`-scoped pattern as kn/hi |
| ED3 | **Gemini-draft → native-speaker review** (inference only, never training; every value flagged for review) | `CLAUDE.md` guardrail | Translation *quality* is human-gated + owner-paced; CI proves parity, not quality |
| ED4 | **CI key-parity + no-bare-strings stay merge gates** for every locale | `hygiene.test.ts` `[verified]` | A new catalog must match the `en` key set exactly or CI fails |
| ED5 | **All 7 are LTR** — no RTL/bidi layout work in this batch | `[researched]` CLDR/W3C | No `dir="rtl"`, no logical-property audit; RTL (ar/he/ur) would be a separate, larger track |
| ED6 *(proposed)* | **Chinese = `zh-Hans` (Simplified) for v1**; `zh-Hant` (Traditional) is a *separate* catalog + font, added later only on demand | §8 Q2 `[design]` | Key the catalog `zh-Hans` (not bare `zh`) so `zh-Hant` slots in cleanly |

## 3. Foundation — research / prior art `[researched]`
Per-language diligence (Unicode CLDR plural-rules chart; SIL OFL; `@fontsource`; W3C BCP-47 — sources in §10):

| Lang | BCP-47 | CLDR **cardinal** plurals | Dir | Dedicated font (OFL) | `@fontsource(-variable)` pkg | Font size / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thai | `th` | **`other`** (1 form) | LTR | **Noto Sans Thai** | `noto-sans-thai` | **small** (single script block) · no-space word-wrap gotcha |
| Indonesian | `id` | **`other`** (1 form) | LTR | — (Latin) | — | pure catalog add |
| Danish | `da` | **`one, other`** (2) | LTR | — (Latin) | — | same shape as English (`æ ø å` in Latin font) |
| Spanish | `es` | **`one, many, other`** (3) ⚠ | LTR | — (Latin) | — | `many` = millions/compact only; ~15–30% string expansion |
| Chinese (Simpl.) | **`zh-Hans`** | **`other`** (1 form) | LTR | **Noto Sans SC** | `noto-sans-sc` | **very large** (~7–16 MB/weight unsubset) · script-variant decision |
| Japanese | `ja` | **`other`** (1 form) | LTR | **Noto Sans JP** | `noto-sans-jp` | **very large** (CJK) |
| Korean | `ko` | **`other`** (1 form) | LTR | **Noto Sans KR** | `noto-sans-kr` | **very large** (Hangul + Han) |

**Three findings that drive the design:**
1. **Spanish's 3-form plural** is the only authoring change. Current CLDR `es` cardinal = `{one, many, other}`; `many` fires only on large/compact numbers (e.g. millions), but every `es` ICU plural message must still carry a `many {…}` arm or `intl-messageformat` falls back. Flag it to the reviewer and extend the parity/lint check. (th/id/zh/ja/ko are **single-form** → trivial; da is 2-form like en.)
2. **Fonts split the batch.** es/da/id need **no new font** (Latin). th needs one **small** OFL font. **zh-Hans/ja/ko need large OFL CJK fonts** — all OFL-1.1 (bundleable under ED2), all on `@fontsource-variable`, but multi-MB each.
3. **CJK + the offline guard collide.** `@fontsource` serves CJK as ~100+ `unicode-range` `woff2` chunks, fetched on demand — efficient *online*, but the SPA's **air-gapped/offline guarantee** (`hygiene.test.ts` "offline / self-contained") means we must ship **all** chunks so any glyph renders with no network → **tens of MB** added with 3 CJK languages. Mitigations in §4.

## 4. Design / architecture
**Reuse map (what's reused vs new):**
- *Reused unchanged:* the negotiation contract (persisted pref → `?lang=` → navigator → `en`), the ICU catalog format, the `@fontsource` `unicode-range`-scoped self-host pattern (`main.tsx:7-8`), the key-parity + no-bare-strings + offline CI (`hygiene.test.ts`), the draft→review pipeline, the `<html lang>` sync (`i18n/index.ts:59-65`).
- *New:* 7 `common.json` catalogs; `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` entries (`i18n/index.ts:18`); **4 new Noto font imports**; an **es 3-form plural** allowance in the catalog + tooling; a **CJK lazy-load + offline-subset** strategy (Phase C); Thai line-break CSS safety net.
- **⚠ Catalog-home caveat `[verified]`:** catalogs today live at `web/src/i18n/locales/{en,kn,hi}/common.json` — **not** a shared top-level `locales/` (the I18N_PLAN "D-CATALOG-HOME" diagram). This expansion adds siblings there. If FRIENDLY **B3** (the shared `site/` `t()` shim) hoists them to a shared dir first, these new catalogs move with them — sequence-aware, not a blocker.

**Negotiation tests (per FRIENDLY §6.5):** every new locale gets an N1/N2-style test — a browser set to that language **auto-renders** it (e.g. `navigator.languages=["ja-JP"]` → `ja`), region subtags normalize (`zh-Hans-CN` → `zh-Hans`), and a missing key falls back to `en`. **Auto-selection is the promise; it's proven by a test, never assumed.**

**Phasing (least new code/assets first):**
- **Phase A — Latin trio (`es`, `da`, `id`): catalog-only, zero new assets.** Highest ROI, proves the pipeline scales beyond Indic with no code. Only `es` needs the 3-form-plural touch.
- **Phase B — Thai (`th`): one small OFL font.** Reuses the kn/hi font pattern exactly; add the no-space word-wrap CSS safety net. Self-contained, low-risk.
- **Phase C — CJK (`zh-Hans`, `ja`, `ko`): the heavy slice.** Needs the zh-Hans/Hant decision, 3 large OFL fonts, **per-locale lazy font-load** (so non-CJK users never download CJK), and an **offline-bundle subset strategy** (ship-all-chunks vs a custom subset build step vs online-lazy-only). One PR per CJK language.

## 5. Privacy / scope note
i18n is **presentation-only** — no new personal-data collection, no biometrics, minors-excluded posture unchanged (same as FRIENDLY §5). Gemini-draft translation is inference, not training, with no user data in the loop. CJK/Thai font bundling is OFL media, not code/weights.

## 6. Honest limits — what this does NOT do
- A green CI / DONE proves **key-parity + render + no-bare-strings + auto-negotiation**, **not** translation *quality* (native-review-gated, ED3) or correct font rendering (owner-eyeball-gated, like kn/hi).
- **`zh-Hant` (Traditional) is out of v1** (separate catalog + font); only `zh-Hans` ships first.
- **No RTL** — this batch is all LTR.
- The **CJK offline bundle is large** (tens of MB) unless a subset build is adopted — a real size trade-off, not free.
- `site/` marketing localization for these 7 **depends on FRIENDLY B3** landing first (the shared shim); until then this is SPA-only.

## 8. Open questions — owner
1. **Approve starting this line** (in parallel with the FRIENDLY UX/A-items), and the **A → B → C phasing**? *(E0 gate.)*
2. **Chinese:** ship **`zh-Hans` only** for v1 (recommended), or **both Hans + Hant**? *(EC0; Hant = a whole extra catalog + Noto Sans TC.)*
3. **CJK offline strategy** *(EC0; the real size trade-off):* (a) ship **all** font chunks → simplest, **+tens of MB** to the air-gapped bundle; (b) a **custom subset build** restricted to glyphs the reviewed catalogs use → KB-scale but adds a build step that re-runs on catalog change; (c) **online lazy-load only** → small bundle but **breaks the offline guarantee for CJK**. *Recommend (b) if offline-for-CJK matters, else (a).*
4. **Native-reviewer sourcing** for 7 languages — who/how/budget? (Vendor like the Roora labeling pilot? community? paid freelancers?) This is the launch-gate cost.
5. **Priority order** — which markets first? (Default A-then-B-then-C is by *engineering cost*; if a market is urgent, e.g. `id` or `ja`, it can jump within its phase.)
6. **Does `site/` localize for these too** for v1, or SPA-only until FRIENDLY B3 lands? *(EX3.)*

## 9. Definition of done
- [ ] All 7 catalogs (`es`, `da`, `id`, `th`, `zh-Hans`, `ja`, `ko`) live + **rendering** on the `web/` SPA; switcher + persistence + `en` fallback; **no raw keys**.
- [ ] **Auto-negotiation green** — a browser set to each language auto-renders it (N1/N2-style tests per locale); region subtags normalize; missing-key → `en`.
- [ ] **CI key-parity + no-bare-strings green** (incl. the `es` 3-form plural) + the **offline/self-contained guard** still green after the CJK fonts land.
- [ ] **Fonts render correctly** — owner eyeball per new script (th, zh-Hans, ja, ko).
- [ ] **Native-speaker review** complete + signed off per language (EX1).
- [ ] CJK **offline/bundle-size** strategy implemented per the EC0 decision; documented bundle-size impact.
- [ ] *(if in scope)* `site/` marketing renders the 7 via the shared shim (EX3, after FRIENDLY B3).
- [ ] §7 all rows ☑ (or explicitly deferred) → flip **Status → DONE** + archive.

### 461. assistant (2026-06-21T14:16:59.803Z)

Proposal is up as **[khelsutra#58](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/58)** (left open for your approval — it's a product/strategy doc, so owner-gated, not auto-merged). Now your conceptual question, which is the important one:

## Can alpha users trigger generation from the UI today? Not yet — but it's closer (and cheaper) than you think

**You *can* trigger generation from the UI** — that's exactly what B-P2 shipped (paste a `gs://` URI → `/api/process` → poll → rallies → compile → download; proven in the screencast). **But you can't share the page with alpha users yet**, for three specific reasons — and **none of them is M12, and none requires a GPU**:

1. **Nothing is hosted for them to reach.** Today the SPA runs via `npm run dev` on *your* machine, pointed at a *local* engine. There's no URL where the SPA **and a reachable engine** live together.
2. **The engine has no auth.** It binds `127.0.0.1:8000`, CORS `*`, single-tenant — unsafe to expose without an **edge-auth** layer in front.
3. **It needs a reachable engine with compute** — and **Gemini is CPU-only**, so a plain CPU box suffices. GPU is only for WASB.

So the blocker is **not M12** (that's the Google-Drive read/write convenience — a nice-to-have, not this) and **not the whole T1–T11** — it's a small subset: **a hosted SPA + engine behind edge auth on a reachable box** = the appliance shell (`LOCAL_APPLIANCE` **T2** + the reverse-proxy topology), plus the screens we already built.

**The cheap path you actually have:** your **existing CPU droplet (`168.144.126.176`) + Gemini can do this with no GPU subscription.** You parked the droplet because the *WASB* CUJ needs a GPU — but **alpha sharing on Gemini doesn't.** That reframes "trigger from the UI for real" from blocked-on-GPU to a handful of ungated steps.

### Recommended priority for the "alpha-shareable UI trigger" goal — after B3/A1/A2/A4/A5/A8
1. **T4 — in-UI local file upload** (the chunked-upload endpoints already exist; the SPA just needs to call them). *This is the one genuinely-missing UX piece:* alpha users have a **video file**, not a `gs://` bucket URI — the current IngestPanel can't take their file yet.
2. **T2 — appliance shell on the droplet:** serve the SPA build + reverse-proxy `/api` → the engine behind **Cloudflare Access** (you already use Cloudflare), engine on **Gemini/CPU**; rotate the chat-shared key.
3. **T11 — retention sweep** + basic hygiene before any non-owner exposure.

That's the alpha-shareable milestone — no GPU, no M12. WASB/GPU and Cloud Run scale-to-zero (the serving session's M7) are *later* "better detection / zero idle cost" upgrades, not prerequisites. (One caveat: the engine processes **one job at a time** in-process — fine for a few alpha users; it queues.)

## So, the execution plan
- ✅ **This turn:** answered the above + shipped the **localization-expansion proposal** ([#58](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/58), awaiting your approval).
- **Next, in order (your go-ahead):** **doc-hygiene cleanup** → **B3** (site/ trilingual shim) → **A1** (plain-language copy) → **A2** (WhatsApp share) → **A4/A5/A8**.
- **Then the goal you flagged:** the **alpha-shareable UI trigger** (T4 upload → T2 droplet+edge-auth+Gemini → T11 retention).

Want me to kick off the doc-hygiene + B3 now, or would you rather I bump **T4 (in-UI upload)** up front since it's the keystone of the "trigger from the UI" goal? Either way I'll hold the 7-language line until you approve #58.

# Session handoff — KhelSutra UX/localization + runnable-tool (refreshed 2026-06-21)

## 1. You are here
The **bucket→reel CUJ is fully UI-driven and proven** (B-P2 shipped + a live screencast in the promo bucket), the **SPA is trilingual (en+kn+hi)**, and the **plain-language copy pass (A1) is merged**. The active line is **`FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md`** (non-tech UX + localization). The **North Star = make the tool runnable/shareable for alpha users from the UI** (a hosted SPA+engine behind edge auth — **NOT** GPU-gated, **NOT** M12; cheapest = the existing CPU droplet + Gemini).

**Merged this session (all on green CI):** engine [#284](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/284) (gs:// regression guard) · [#289](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/289) (compile-from-bucket fix) · khelsutra [#55](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/55) (stale-doc fix) · [#56](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/56) (B-P2 in-UI ingest) · [#57](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/57) (DoD §6 box3 ☑) · [#59](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/59) (doc-hygiene) · [#60](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/60) (A1 copy). **Owner-merged:** [#58](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/58) (MULTILINGUAL_EXPANSION proposal — 7-lang line **approved**). Screencast: `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/promo/khelsutra-bucket-cuj-screencast.mp4`.

## 2. Next steps — ordered (merge each on green CI + rising coverage; one PR per item, off `origin/master`)
**A) Finish the SPA UX A-items, then B3** *(active `FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md` line; A1 ✅, A2 DROPPED):*
- **A4 — mobile touch targets + visible tooltip text.** Files: `web/src/components/{QueryBar,RallyGrid,SelectionFooter,IngestPanel,CompileResult,LanguageSwitcher}.tsx` + `index.css`. Bump interactive elements to ≥44px tap targets (`min-h-11`/py), enlarge the tiny query "parsed-pill" + sample chips, and surface `title=` tooltips as visible helper text on phones. Effort M.
- **A5 — friendly engine-error mapping (build path).** Reuse the existing `web/src/lib/errors.ts::classifyError` (built for ingest) on the **build/compile** path: `App.tsx::onBuild` currently shows raw `ApiError` text for 5xx/network. Add a **shared `errors.*` i18n namespace** (recommend unifying IngestPanel onto it too), map onBuild's catch → localized message in `CompileResult`. Effort S–M. New keys.
- **A8 — background-processing reassurance + status as icon+text chips.** `IngestPanel` progress + the build state: reassurance copy ("can take a few minutes on slow connections") + render queued/running/done/failed as icon **+ text** chips (never icon-only). Effort S–M. New keys.
- **B3 — `site/` trilingual via a shared `t()` shim** (carry the trilingual SPA win to the marketing site, which is still English-only). New ~30–50-line `t(key,vars)`/`data-i18n` shim over `site/public/{index,capture,own-model}.html`; `site/server.js` Accept-Language negotiation; extend `site/src/assets.test.js`. **DECISION (D-CATALOG-HOME):** hoist `web/src/i18n/locales/{en,kn,hi}/common.json` to a shared top-level `locales/` consumed by BOTH surfaces (recommended), vs the site importing/copying from `web/src/i18n/locales`. Effort M. *(Catalogs live under `web/src/i18n/locales/` today, NOT a shared dir — B3 resolves this.)*

**B) ▶ NORTH STAR — runnable tool for alpha users (the crucial fresh-context work):**
- **T4 — in-UI local file UPLOAD (the keystone).** Alpha users have a **video file**, not a `gs://` URI. Wire the SPA to the **existing** engine chunked-upload endpoints (`backend/api/uploads.py`: `/api/upload/init|part|complete|DELETE`) + a file-picker UI in/beside `IngestPanel` → on complete, hand the server path to the existing `/api/process` → poll → load → compile → download flow. Net-new: `web/src/api/client.ts` upload helpers + an upload mode; reused: the whole back-half. Effort M–L.
- **T2 — droplet appliance shell (hosted + shareable).** Serve the built SPA + reverse-proxy `/api` → engine `127.0.0.1:8000` behind **Cloudflare Access** (edge auth) on the **CPU droplet `168.144.126.176`**, engine on **Gemini/CPU (NO GPU)**. Deploy the #281/#289-fixed engine + `GEMINI_API_KEY` + `pip install google-cloud-storage`; **rotate the chat-shared GEMINI_API_KEY**. Reuse `site/scripts/provision-vm.sh` + `docs/PORTABILITY.md`. Owner step: Cloudflare Access config. Effort M–L (+owner).
- **T11 — retention sweep** for `output/` + `uploads/` originals before any non-owner exposure. Effort S. Owner: retention window.

**C) 7-language line — APPROVED (#58), parallel track, owner-paced:** `MULTILINGUAL_EXPANSION.md` §7. **Phase A (`es`/`da`/`id`) is actionable now** (catalog-only; **`es` needs the 3-form ICU plural `{one, many, other}`** + a parity/lint touch). **Phase B (Thai)** = 1 small OFL font. **Phase C (`zh-Hans`/`ja`/`ko`)** = large OFL fonts + lazy-load + offline-subset — **blocked on owner Q2/Q3**. Native review (Q4) is the launch gate.

## 3. Open owner decisions (unblock the most)
- **7-lang (in `MULTILINGUAL_EXPANSION.md` §8):** Q2 ship `zh-Hans` only or +`zh-Hant`? · Q3 CJK offline-bundle strategy (ship-all-chunks +tens-of-MB / custom-subset build / online-lazy-only) · Q4 native-reviewer sourcing for 7 langs · Q5 priority order.
- **B3:** D-CATALOG-HOME (shared top-level `locales/` — recommended).
- **T2:** Cloudflare Access setup + **GEMINI_API_KEY rotation**. **T11:** retention window. Native kn/hi review + owner-machine font eyeball (the existing trilingual launch gates).

## 4. Ramp-up kit (read in this order)
1. This file → memory `current-state.md` (today's checkpoints).
2. `khelsutra/docs/FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md` §7 (the active UX/l10n tracker) + `DRY_RUN_CUJ.md` §9 (the delivered CUJ) + `LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §9 T2/T4/T11 + §4 topology (the North Star) + `MULTILINGUAL_EXPANSION.md` (the approved 7-lang plan).
3. Code: `khelsutra/web/src/{App.tsx, components/IngestPanel.tsx, lib/errors.ts, api/client.ts, i18n/index.ts, i18n/hygiene.test.ts}`; `badminton-highlight-indexer/backend/api/uploads.py` (the upload endpoints for T4).
4. A `wf_b7edcaa8` spec workflow produced detailed B3/A4/A5/A8/North-Star tickets (re-run `docs-next-steps`/`wrapup-specs` or check the workflow transcript if more file:line detail is wanted).

## 5. Key decisions — don't relitigate
- **A2 (WhatsApp/social share) DROPPED** → owner defers ALL social/sharing to a separate scope; reel stays **pure download** (wa.me can't attach files; real file-share = Web Share API = own scope).
- **Runnable-tool path = hosted SPA+engine behind edge auth; cheapest = droplet + Gemini/CPU. NOT GPU-gated, NOT M12.** (The droplet was parked only for the WASB/GPU CUJ; Gemini alpha needs no GPU.)
- **B-P1 was already shipped** (engine `/api/process` accepts `gs://` since M2/#280) — see [[lesson-verify-engine-surface-before-scoping]]; verify engine-surface claims at file:line.
- **DON'T touch the serving track** — `COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING` / `backend/compute/` / Cloud-Run M5–M12 is a **concurrent session's** active line (M1–M8 merged, M9 #290 open). Engine doc-staleness (DOC_STATUS/NEXT_STEPS/RALLY_QUALITY n) was left for that session. **Shared checkout** → branch from `origin/master`, re-verify HEAD before commit, stage explicit paths ([[gotcha-shared-checkout-branch-collisions]]).
- **Merge authorization:** Claude merges its own green good-engineering/UX PRs on verified CI ([[working-agreement-merging]]); product/strategy/licensing + the 7-lang Phase-C/native-review stay owner-gated.
